# PRISM window redesign, stage 5 — Checks, Inference and the Narrowing round: Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** "Parameter inference" is final: step 4, Checks (twelve checks on one list, each with its form, its run and its result, under the headline "n of 6 deciding checks passed"), step 5, Inference, and step 6, the Narrowing round, are built on stage 4's step page; one press of a plan fills every step; Training's "Before you start" states the pre-run checks; the comparison of unusable driven recordings is made when simulation ends; and a narrowing round kept partway can be resumed, or continued from its last saved batch, after PRISM is reopened.

**Architecture:** The three pages are `StepPage`s (`core/gui/panels/inference/step_page.py`) and keep their class and attribute names (`ValidatePanel`/`validate_panel` becomes the Checks list, `InferPanel`/`infer_panel`, `TSNPEPanel`/`tsnpe_panel`), as stage 4 kept `ConfigPanel`, `PriorPanel` and `PosteriorPanel`. What a check is, how its record is read, which records count and the headline are pure functions with no toolkit and no torch, in flat modules beside `core/gui/step_facts.py` (`check_catalogue.py`, `check_readings.py`, `check_lineage.py`; the scratch store of the two checks that keep nothing, `check_scratch.py`); the list, a check's own page, its form and its result block are toolkit code under `core/gui/panels/inference/`. The run's record and model learn the comparison (the FIRST masked-probes record with its scope, the health row's state, "needs you", the history's `check` and `health` entries, a public amendment of an ended run's history). The engine gains E4 (`observation_name`) and the rest of E6 (six refusals given the field `posterior`); no function of the named training code is touched.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.12, PySide6 6.9.3, matplotlib 3.10.7, torch 2.9.0+cu130, sbi 0.26.1, pytest; the conda env `biophys-env`.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-window-redesign-design.md` (approved 2026-10-06; its section 8, departures 1 to 397 and this plan's 398 onward, wins over the parts where they differ; rows 126 to 397 are also in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-30-window-redesign-stage-4.md`, some only there) with its parts in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-window-redesign-design/`: `09-checks.md` (whole), `08-steps.md` (8.1, 8.4's pre-run checks line, 8.5, 8.6, and its choices), `05-anatomy.md`, `07-plans.md` (7.1, 7.3), `06-run.md` (6.2, 6.3.1, 6.3.3 to 6.3.5, 6.5, 6.8), `03-shell.md` (the rail's marks, Home's next step and "Continue with these"), `11-engine.md` (11.4 E3, 11.5 E4, 11.7 E6, 11.8, 11.11 the registry), `04-look.md`, `12-findings.md`, `13-tests-stages-docs.md` (13.1, 13.2.2's "Stage 5", 13.3, 13.4); the locked records `…-assets/decisions-as-approved.md` and `…-assets/rulings-after-the-drafts.md` (the later wins). THE FIT-SAVING DESIGN `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-21-fit-saving-design.md` (4.2, 4.6, 6.2, 8) WINS over the redesign wherever the redesign says a stopped fit is lost (`09-checks.md`'s "stopping loses only the hours of fitting so far" and 8.6's fit line among them), with its last review's round rows (`.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-06-window-redesign/fit-saving/reviews/last-review-two.md`). Every task cites the sections it implements; those sections bind except where "Departures from the design" says otherwise. The evidence this plan was written from is in the gitignored workspace `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-06-window-redesign/stage-5/plan-drafts/`: `reader-a-checks.md`, `reader-b-inference.md`, `reader-c-round.md`, `reader-d-engine.md`, `reader-e-tests.md`, `reader-f-carried.md` (each at HEAD `cdc80d2`) and `probes-report.md`. Stages 1 to 4's plans hold the registry of every name those stages built; this plan names only what it adds or changes.

## For the owner: what this plan does, in plain words

**What you have at its end.** Every page of Parameter inference is built the same way: a heading and one plain sentence, what is in use, the few main settings with the rest folded under "Rarely changed", "Before you start", name and note, and one blue button named for what it does.

**Checks** (step 4) lists all twelve checks in four groups ("Before the run", "After simulating", "After the fit", "Tools"), each with its role ("Decides", "Informs", "Tool"), its state and when it last ran. The headline is the only large line: "4 of 6 deciding checks passed", or, for a posterior trained with χ off, "2 of 3 deciding checks passed; 3 do not apply to this posterior" (your answer of today). Choosing a check opens its page beside the list (in its place when the window is narrow): its question in one sentence, its state, its button ("Run the calibration", "Run again"), greyed with its reason in words when it cannot run, then its result from its record: a sentence in the window's words, a table, the figures. A record counts only for the posterior or prior it was made for, matched by id; the newest counts, and the row says when an earlier one came out differently. The timing of a short fit and the card test run in a scratch folder that is removed afterwards; only their result is kept, in the run history. Nothing on a check's form is remembered between launches.

**After simulating.** When a training's simulation ends, the window compares its share of unusable driven recordings with the audit of the same prior: within 3 points is fine; 3 to 5 says "look closer" and offers "Accept this difference", kept with its date; above 5 advises stopping the fit, with "Stop training…" and "Keep training", and the page and the rail's foot say the run needs you until you choose. The window never stops the fit itself. With no audit, or a total that covers only part of the set, it says so and asks nothing.

**Training's "Before you start"** gains one line on the frequency band and the audit for the prior in use: "Fine." when both passed, "Attention." when one is not run or failed, "Information." when they do not apply. It never greys "Start training"; after a failed one, the start asks one question first, with Cancel preselected.

**Inference** (step 5) has two clearly separate modes, "A simulated cell" and "Your own recordings". The driven recordings are a table you can plan from the spontaneous recording, each row with its reading ("Fine, 23 drive cycles"). The observation must be named before the run (the inference's own name is optional). With a narrowed posterior, every run asks "Run on another observation?" first. Afterwards: a table of each parameter's 5 %, 50 % and 95 % values with units (and the true value for a simulated cell), the corner plot, and "Narrow around this observation".

**Narrowing round** (step 6) opens on the observation just made, shows and passes its network settings (today it silently trains 128 by 8), says what it will continue, and asks only when a saved set one setting away exists. Afterwards it says how much of the prior the region kept and offers "Check this posterior". **A round stopped partway** is listed on this page after a reopening, with Resume and Delete, saying what a Resume will load (your answer of today); Home's next line and the rail's mark lead to it; a failed round offers "Continue from batch N".

**Plans** now fill every step with one press, the calibration's sizes, the inference's draws and the round's settings included.

**What does not change.** No model, simulation, feature, training or calibration method; no setting in `core/config.py`; no simulation set's identity or what a row holds (`Artifacts/simulations/eda5c2a1e468` stays usable); a fit that is never interrupted computes exactly what it computes today; nothing under `Artifacts/`. The numbers a deciding check is judged by (37 % within 8 points for the audit, 3 and 5 points for the comparison, and the rest of part 9.2) are written as named constants in the window's checks code, each with its reason, and go on the code audit's list as science numbers living outside the engine.

**How big it is.** Thirteen building tasks and two that I run myself, against the design's estimate of twelve. Stage 4 had fourteen, held to them, and grew by fix rounds (Tasks 7, 9 and 12 needed two each), one fix wave after its last review, and three small tasks at its exit; it took seven chats after its plan's, from 30 October to 9 November, and a little more than one weekly bar. I expect the same here: about one fix round per task, so roughly twenty-six to thirty build-and-review cycles, a last review with its wave, and small tasks at the exit; 50 to 65 million tokens in background agents; about seven or eight chats, handed over every two or three tasks. Every fast gate now takes about 45 minutes (stage 4's exit, 43 min 28 s at 3572 tests) and this stage dictates about 195 new test functions, about 590 collected items, so expect 50 to 55 minutes by its end. It starts in this week (the bar read 23 % at the plan's dispatch, resetting on 15 November at 04:00 UTC) and will run into the next; I stop at a clean state whenever the bar nears 90 %.

**What needs your hand.**
1. *Now:* your yes to this plan, with its choices below.
2. *During the build:* the few questions a task meets that touch what you decided, asked one at a time while a gate runs.
3. *At the exit:* a quiet machine for the fast gate of record; THE SLOW SET, owed now for stages 4 and 5 together (1 h 0 min 49 s alone at stage 3's exit; the machine reads slower since); the card test (below; I tell you first); the real-screen run (hidden windows only); the pictures; the walkthrough rows written for you; the guide's pages for Checks, Inference and the round; your yes or no to the `CLAUDE.md` sentences this stage makes false (the inference pages' memory, "Drive F₀ (N)", the suites and counts), each in exact words; and the list of everything I decided without you.

**The card test is owed, once, at the exit; so is the slow set.** The card test follows changes that touch training or move tensors: a round's Resume starts a training on the card through a new path; the timing check trains two short fits from the window; the card test itself becomes a check run from the window. So the exit runs `CLAUDE.md`'s five command lines and run 5. The diagnostic card is not owed by its rule: the six refusals of E6 sit under `core/diagnostics` but move no tensor (they are raised before anything is spent). I run it anyway at the exit, because two of its commands (`ablation`, `identifiability laplace`) exercise the very functions those refusals change, end to end on real records; it adds about six minutes. A card-marked test resumes a tiny round through the window's own call.

**What I will do on your computer beyond editing code and running tests.** The trial runs before each task build PRISM's window hidden, on scratch settings files and scratch stores, never yours. Tiny trainings on the processor in the tests and on the card in the fast gate's card tests and the card gates; no training of real size; nothing under your real `Artifacts/` is touched (Checks only reads records). Nothing signs you out, locks or shuts down the computer, and nothing writes into your real desktop folder.

**The named training code is not touched.** No task edits any of the seventeen named functions (the round's resume, E4 and E6 all lie outside them), so a kept training stays resumable across this stage. If a task finds it would have to, it stops and asks you first.

**Engine changes beyond E4 and E6,** each outside the named code, each on the code audit's list: the narrowing round's one refusal that names no field (a narrowed posterior given another observation) gains the field `observation`, so the page can say where to fix it. Nothing else: a resumed round's saved share of the prior was feared to read 0 % (a reading of the source); a trial showed it reads what an uninterrupted round reads (99.323 % against 99.324 % at the same seed), so no change is made. Two faults the trials found go on the audit's list, not into this stage: an observation given twice is saved twice, and an inference refused for another observation has already saved that observation.

**Choices in this plan that you have not seen.** Approving the plan approves these unless you strike one ("Departures from the design" below has each with what it costs if it is wrong), together with every "choice the owner has not seen" that the design's parts 3, 5, 6, 7, 8 and 9 list for these pages (`reader-f-carried.md` section 6 groups them by page). The ones you would notice:
- *A check made at less than its usual size never decides*: a calibration on fewer than 2,000 test datasets or 200 operating points, an audit of fewer than 100 batches, and the card test's own small calibration; the row stays "Not run yet" and says so.
- *"Keep training"*, answered when the comparison advises stopping, is kept once with its date and shown on the check's page; it is not asked again at a reopening.
- *Enter on a driven recording's remove button asks first* when the row holds anything typed ("Remove this driven recording?", with "Keep it" preselected); your rule that Enter presses the ringed button stays window-wide.
- *The physical drive's box reads "Drive amplitude" (N)*, as the design's page table has it; `CLAUDE.md`'s "Drive F₀ (N)" follows at the exit on your yes.
- *The card test's "Before you start" says the prior is built at full size* (the design's "builds a small prior" is not what the engine does: its prior stage is the full stability sweep, about two of its six minutes on your card).
- *A round's time estimate* names earlier narrowing rounds, never "the number given on the Training page"; a first round says it has no earlier round to estimate from.
- *A kept round's Resume* puts its own posterior, prior and observation in use, asking first when something else is in use, as a training's Resume does.
- *The Checks code lives in small files beside stage 4's* rather than in a folder of its own: a new folder would slip past four of the standing scans.
- *A check's page stands beside the list only in a wide window* (from about 1,160 px at the ordinary text size), and the list then shows only each check's name and state; narrower, the check's page takes the list's place, with "All checks" to go back. The design's full table beside a page needs a window about 1,450 px wide at the ordinary text size and never fits at 150 %.
- *The timing of a short fit is two runs*, one per network, started by one press, the second when the first ends; the run history holds two entries. One run cannot show two fits.
- *With a narrowed posterior and your own recordings, the question says what PRISM really does*: "If these are the same recordings with the same description, PRISM finds them to be that observation and the inference runs as on it; your answer is then not recorded, though the observation is saved again as a new record." (The design's "nothing is recorded" is not so: a trial saved the same recordings twice.)
- *The advice above 5 points says a stop keeps the fit after its last saved epoch* (the fit is saved after every epoch and can be resumed), where the design said stopping "loses only the hours of fitting so far".
- *A new posterior keeps the observation in use, a new prior lets it go*: the observation is the posterior's data, not its product.

## Global Constraints

- **Interpreter.** `C:\Users\J\anaconda3\envs\biophys-env\python.exe` (in Bash `/c/Users/J/anaconda3/envs/biophys-env/python.exe`); the `python` on PATH has none of the dependencies. The root `conftest.py` defaults `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen` and `KMP_DUPLICATE_LIB_OK=TRUE` for pytest; a bare script sets what it needs. A foreground check that imports torch can hang: write it to a script and run it with a timeout.
- **Git.** The local `main`; one commit per logical step (a fix after review is its own commit), never amended; a short subject; the message ends with the Co-Authored-By line the harness provides; `git add` each changed file by name; read `git status` first; undo with `git stash`, never `git checkout -- <file>`. The owner pushes.
- **Suites.** Never edit a file under `core/`, `tests/`, the root `conftest.py`, `run.bat` or `run.sh` while a pytest process is alive; never two pytest processes, scratch copies included. An implementer runs only its focused tests (one file or a few tests, one process; in the background with a log when a run can pass ten minutes). The controller runs each task's fast gate (`python -m pytest -m "not slow" -q --durations=10`, one process, background, logged: about 45 minutes at stage 4's exit) and starts it with the task's review. Readers run no python while a gate runs.
- **THE OWNER'S LINES** (each binds every task; a task that would cross one STOPS and reports): the science (models, simulation, features, training and calibration methods, every setting in `core/config.py`) does not change; what a simulated row holds and the simulation set's identity (`SimulationIdentity`) do not change, and `_canonical`, `identity_digest` and `resolve_dir` are not touched, since they name every set's folder; a fit that is never interrupted computes exactly what it computes today; `CLAUDE.md` is edited only on the owner's yes; nothing under `Artifacts/` and not `C:\Users\J\prism-retrain-console.log` is renamed, rewritten, deleted or trained on; no training of real size; nothing signs the owner out, locks or shuts down the computer, or writes into the real desktop folder. No check's reading rule (the numbers a deciding check is judged by, 9.2) is a setting of `core/config.py`: each is a named constant of `core/gui/check_readings.py` with its reason in words, and goes on the code audit's list as a science number living outside the engine (B10).
- **THE FIRM REQUIREMENT** (departure 125, the owner's words): "at the window's least size, and at every width above it, nothing of the run page is hidden under the form; the form and the run page share the width with neither drawn over the other." Held by `test_nothing_of_the_run_page_is_hidden_under_the_form_at_any_width` (`tests/test_run_states.py`), at 900 x 600 and every width up to the opening size, at text factors 1.0 and 1.5, and by `test_nothing_of_the_run_page_is_hidden_at_150_percent_display_scaling`, in a process of its own under `QT_SCALE_FACTOR=1.5`. The rebuilt Checks list with a check's page beside it, Inference and the Narrowing round join both tests' legs in the task that builds each. Windows' own scaling on the real platform is read in the exit's pictures.
- **The named training code.** An edit inside a function `core.SBI.fit_state.FIT_CODE` names (seventeen; `core/SBI/fit_state.py:88-106`: `core.SBI.train`'s `train_nn`, `fit_settings`, `build_trainer`, `prepare_fit`, `run_fit`, `take_snapshot`, `restore_trainer`, `_capped_zscore_check`; `core.SBI.embedded_network`'s `_probit`, `_column_quantiles`, `EmbeddedNet`; `core.SBI.chi_encoder.ChiSetEncoder`; `core.SBI.summaries.winsorize_summary_block`; `core.SBI.chi`'s `band_norm`, `n_chi_features`; `core.orchestrator`'s `build_embedding_net`, `expected_forcing_dim`), a comment or a docstring included, makes every kept training unresumable. NO TASK EDITS ONE. A task that finds it must STOPS and the controller asks the owner first. `test_the_named_training_code_is_as_recorded` fails first if one moves. The last statement of `core/orchestrator.py` stays `fit_state.remember_loaded_code()` (about `:3103`), so a new engine function goes above it. Keep each edited engine file's line endings as they are (the digests read them).
- **Scope.** Engine files change only in Task 2 and only as it lists: `core/orchestrator.py` (E4 in `simulated_inference` and `experimental_inference`; `field="observation"` on `tsnpe_round`'s refusal of a narrowed posterior given another observation), `core/diagnostics/ablation.py` and `core/diagnostics/identifiability.py` (E6's six refusals), `core/refusals.py`, `core/tool/fields.py`, `core/plans.py` (the new key's class). Every other task stays under `core/gui/`, `tests/`, and the documents the exit lists; `Resources/` never. Every engine change goes on the code audit's list (`docs/superpowers/notes/2026-10-29-the-code-audits-list.md`) and the ledger's "Engine changes landed" in the task that makes it. `core.refusals.FIELDS` holds 114 keys today; this stage makes it 115 (`observation_name`). A key added touches both front ends' tables, the count pin, `BASE_KEYS` and `core/plans.py`'s classes in one commit. A task that finds it must open another engine file STOPS and the controller brings it to the owner.
- **Knobs.** Every knob reaches its stage as an argument; no task writes `core.config` (the memory limit's one writer stays `core/gui/memory_limit.py`). Every count, delay or limit a task names (a reading rule's number, a layout threshold, a debounce) is a named constant, passed as an argument where a test must shorten it.
- **NEVER READ WHILE A RUN IS LIVE** (stage 4's rule, probe 6 of stage 4: a reader in a tight loop made 4 of 18 saves of a simulation set fail). No page, refresh or chain reads the store's sets, the saved simulations (`peek`), the kept trainings, the card, the preview or the run history between a run's start and its end. The Checks list's reading of manifests joins the counted readings (`COUNTED_READINGS`, `tests/_fixtures.py`) under its own name; while a run is live the list shows what it read last and check 3 shows the live training's comparison from the run's model in memory, reading no file (9.5); a cell file's check (`cli.validate_gt_file`) moves into the page's `read_disk`. A picker's listing of priors, posteriors and observations is no such reading. The controller's order is load-bearing: the status stops being live before `status_changed`, and `status_changed` before `run_ended` (`core/gui/run/controller.py` about `:1203`, `:1232`, `:1233`); a page's re-listing at `run_ended` relies on it, and a test pins it.
- **Facts.** Every page's facts and every check's reading come from a function that takes values and no widget, in a module with no toolkit and no torch, tested as values. Every "Error." and "Blocked." blocks, and nothing else does; a marker never disagrees with the button. A constructor reads no card, no simulation set, no kept training and no manifest, starts no program and starts no timer.
- **The stage-4 exit's answers on every new page.** A reason that waiting will not lift begins "Cannot start:", one that a run's end will lift "Cannot start yet:" (A5); the low window's "Show all N" as stage 4 built it (answer 13); through the shared foot, tested on the stand-in page in Task 4.
- **Pages hand a run names and numbers only**, never a record or an array (`history.plain` copes with nothing else); a round's run hands its history `settings` as names and numbers (model, options, bounds, prior id, parent posterior id, observation id, level, directions, the budget and network rows).
- **Clocks and threads in tests.** No real sleep: every delay and estimate reads an injected clock or a timer a test can fire. A live run in a window test is the stand-in run of `tests/_runs.py`, never a flag poked on a panel; every test that starts a run waits for its end before it returns. No `tiny_run` in a new suite unless the test trains for real: a test that wants a record writes one with the fixtures.
- **Windows calls.** Keep-awake, the reason against shutting down and the taskbar flash reach Windows ONLY through `core/gui/this_computer.py`; the session-wide guard `windows_asked` stands.
- **Dialogs.** Every question on a rebuilt page is the shared `ask` or `ask_text`; a rebuilt page keeps no `QMessageBox` (the scan reads `core/gui/panels/inference`, which is why the Checks pages live there). A real `exec()` off-screen stalls the run: a test answers through the guard. A picture script never shows a dialog, even hidden: it renders it.
- **THE REFERENCE RULE.** Nothing written into `core/`, `tests/`, `conftest.py`, `README.md`, `run.bat`, `run.sh` or `docs/guide/` (code, comment, docstring, on-screen string, test name, batch-file remark) may cite a working document or its labels: no design id (U16, B1, C15, E4, W057, G26 and the like: `tests/test_source_hygiene.py` refuses a lone capital letter with one to three digits), no "ruling", no "Task N", "section N.M", "walkthrough", "ledger", "STATE.md", "CLAUDE.md", "finding N", "the review of"; also forbidden though no scan catches them: "part 9", "stage 5", "the design", "the mock-up", "departure N", "gate 3" (the runbook's gates are named by what they check), any plug-in's name. Say the reason in words. A test's name never holds a token of one letter and one to three digits between underscores (check numbers are words: `check_four`, or the check's name).
- **Two scans a new module can trip.** Under `core/`, a string literal passed to ANY call as a keyword named `field` or `key`, or as the first argument of anything named `require_*`, `describe` or `refuse`, must be a registered refusal key: `remembered.put` and every settings key are passed positionally, never as `key=`. No call to a bare name `input`.
- **No torch and no toolkit where none is needed.** `core/gui/check_catalogue.py`, `check_readings.py`, `check_lineage.py`, `step_facts.py` and `continuing.py` import neither PySide6 nor torch (a fresh-interpreter test closes each import set); `chain.py` reads the checks' summary through them, usable before the Checks page is built (B21).
- **The builders and their scans.** A window, a part or a step is built in a suite only through `tests/_fixtures.py`'s builders; a new public class with a base under `core/gui/*.py`, `screens/`, `panels/` or `run/` is named in the builders' scan (`tests/test_settings_persistence.py`, about `:1185-1216`) in the commit that adds it; NO NEW FOLDER under `core/gui/` (the folder list is pinned exactly). A page built on the step page is added by hand to every list that names the rebuilt pages, in the commit that builds it: `PAGES`, `EDITED`, `WALKED` (`tests/test_pages.py`), `REBUILT` (`tests/test_applied_and_pending.py`), the module list and the step set (`tests/test_nav_and_gating.py`, about `:4203`, `:4284-4286`, `:4346-4347`), `COUNTED_READINGS` (`tests/_fixtures.py`), `PLACES` (`tests/_runs.py`); `test_the_pages_walked_here_are_every_page_built_on_the_step_page` is the net. The drawing guard (`@reported`) covers every method the toolkit calls while a page is drawn or laid out.
- **Process-wide state.** A task that adds something to the process adds a key to `process_state(app)`, its putting-back to `whole_program()` and to the per-test teardown in the same commit.
- **The look's suite and its devices.** A device a page draws with is used as stage 1 built it; read stage 2's registry line "Handed-in lengths" before passing a length. A new painted device is tested in `tests/test_look.py` under its rules (run the WHOLE file before the commit). A rebuilt page's number field asks for equal-width digits, right alignment and the 120 px width, and its whole-number boxes group (`grouped=True`): the owner's "page by page" of 6 October. A middle never begins with a control. Access keys only on a page's main button and heading buttons.
- **Old pages stay as they are** but for what a task lists. After this stage the old frame (`BasePanel`) remains only under the parts stage 7 rebuilds; `_StagePanel`, `_CellPreviewMixin` and `_TrainingBudgetMixin` go with their last page, in the task that rebuilds it, with `base.py`'s docstrings ("six tabs", the code audit's item 25).
- **The carried-over list** (`.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-06-window-redesign/carried-over.md`) is kept current by the controller after every task; the new suite's tests get rows in its appendix.
- **Scratch.** Scratch scripts, the gate script and the picture script under `C:\Users\J\AppData\Local\Temp\prism-redesign-scratch\` (stage 5's in `s5\`); no `.py` under `.superpowers/` or `docs/`; text copies of tools as `stage-5/tools/<name>.py.txt`. A script that imports torch sets `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=-1` (an empty value does not hide the card here) unless it is a card run the controller has announced. Trial copies by `git -c core.autocrlf=false archive`, `run.sh`'s 100755 restored.
- **Real platform.** A step that runs on the real platform creates hidden windows only, NEVER ACTIVATES one, reads Windows' foreground before and after, and runs with no pytest alive.
- **Qt facts.** What Qt can do is checked in the installed PySide6 6.9.3 or Qt's public documentation, with a scratch script.

## Review Focus

Conditions the design implies, that no task's tests would otherwise exercise, and that a person will meet; each line's test is in the task that owns the code.

1. **A record that moves under Checks.** A check's record or the posterior in use is deleted or renamed in Records, or a manifest cannot be read (torn, a field missing, an older shape): the list and the check's page read again, say it in words, raise nothing, and a renamed record still counts (matched by id): Task 5, `test_a_record_that_moves_or_cannot_be_read_is_said_and_raises_nothing`.
2. **The comparison at the edges of its counts.** None counted (a total of 0), a part of the set, a training continued after a reopening that simulates nothing, an audit made at fewer than 100 batches, an audit written after the training started, a set with no history: never a division by zero, always "Not compared", "Waiting for the audit" or a band in words: Task 3, `test_the_comparison_at_the_edges_of_its_counts`; Task 9, `test_a_continued_training_that_simulates_nothing_is_still_compared`.
3. **A check that ends any way but well.** Refused, failed, stopped, or pressed twice while starting: the state stays as it was, nothing is kept, no second run starts; a check in a scratch store removes its folder however it ends, and the timing's second run never starts after the first did not finish: Task 6, `test_a_check_that_ends_any_way_but_well_leaves_the_state_as_it_was`; Task 8, `test_the_scratch_folder_is_removed_however_the_check_ends`.
4. **A kept round whose world has moved.** After a reopening its parent posterior, its prior or its observation was deleted, its name was taken by another posterior, or the same round was started again from zero meanwhile (probe 4: that leaves the kept training behind and takes its posterior's name): the page says which before anything is read; nothing raises; Resume only where the record can be resumed; a taken name asks for a name: Task 13, `test_a_kept_round_whose_records_have_moved_says_why_before_anything_is_read`, `test_a_kept_round_whose_name_was_taken_asks_for_a_name`.
5. **The narrowest window with everything on it.** At 900 x 600, at text 1.0 and 1.5: a check's page with its fold open and a field in error, Inference with twelve driven recordings and one row in error, the round with every fact: the main button and its reason stay inside the window and over nothing, and Tab reaches every field in view: Tasks 4, 5, 10, 11 join `test_the_foot_stays_over_nothing_at_the_smallest_window` and `test_tab_walks_the_page_in_view_at_every_size`.

## Names tasks share (the registry)

Each task's section gives the exact signatures it produces; the names below are binding across tasks.

- **R-engine (Task 2).** `orchestrator.simulated_inference(..., observation_name: str = "")` and `orchestrator.experimental_inference(..., observation_name: str = "")`, checked before anything is spent; `core.refusals.FIELDS` gains `observation_name` (115); the six E6 refusals `Refusal(<their sentence>, field="posterior")`; `tsnpe_round`'s refusal of another observation `field="observation"`.
  - *Signatures.* `simulated_inference(cfg, posterior, T_obs_s, *, cell=None, gt_values=None, prior=None, accept=None, n_samples=None, name="", note="", observation_name: str = "", fig_sink=None, store=None, if_name_taken="refuse")`; `experimental_inference(cfg, posterior, rec, *, accept=None, n_samples=None, name="", note="", observation_name: str = "", fig_sink=None, store=None, if_name_taken="refuse")`; both return `(LoadedObservation, LoadedInference)` as today. The check: `store.assert_name_free("observation", observation_name)` right after the inference's own, re-raised as `Refusal(str(exc), field="observation_name")`; the forward `name=observation_name` to `generate_observations` / `build_experiment_observation`, always. `Field("observation_name", "the observation's name", None)`; `core.tool.fields.FLAG["observation_name"] = None`; `core.gui.fields.CONTROL["observation_name"]`: `None` (Task 2), `("infer", "Name of the observation")` (Task 10); `core.plans`' "it belongs to one run": `name`, `note`, `seed`, `observation_name`. E6: `raise Refusal(<the sentence of today>, field="posterior")` at `ablation.py:41`, `:105`, `:115`, `:130`, `:132` and `identifiability.py:238-241`.
- **R-checks (Task 3).** `core/gui/check_catalogue.py` (no toolkit, no torch): `@dataclass(frozen=True) class CheckEntry` (`key`, `number`, `name`, `sentence`, `group`, `role`, `button`, `stop_words`, `kind`, `diagnostic`, `variant`), `CHECKS` (the twelve, in 9.1's order), `GROUPS`, `ROLES`, `DECIDING` (six keys), `entry(key)`. The keys, binding in every later task and test: `band`, `audit`, `comparison`, `calibration`, `input_use`, `identifiability`, `pin_down`, `timing`, `fixed_number`, `card_test`, `drive_study`, `across_prior`. `core/gui/check_readings.py`: `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Reading` (`state`, `words`, `counts`, …), `STATES`, B10's named constants, `read(key, manifest) -> Reading`, `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Comparison` (`difference`, `state`, `sentence`, `message`), `compare(*, unusable, total, scope, audit_fraction, accepted=None, batches=None, prior_words="the prior in use", kept_set=True) -> Comparison`. `core/gui/check_lineage.py`: `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Held` (what is in use, as ids and plain values), `@dataclass(frozen=True) class CheckRow`, `rows(held, records, histories) -> tuple[CheckRow, ...]`, `headline(rows, *, held) -> tuple[str, str]`, `@dataclass(frozen=True) class ChecksSummary` (what 3.6 reads: `passed`, `of`, `failed`, `look_closer`, `open`, `can_check`, `second_line`, `headline`, `sentence`), `summary(rows, *, held) -> ChecksSummary`, `pre_run_fact(rows) -> Fact`.
  - *Signatures.* (Every function and class of the three modules is handed a check's key positionally, and `CHECKS` is built positionally, so no literal reaches a `key=` keyword: the head's two scans.) `CheckEntry(key: str, number: int, name: str, sentence: str, group: str, role: str, button: str, stop_words: str, kind: str, diagnostic: str | None, variant: str | None)`; `CHECKS: tuple[CheckEntry, ...]`; `GROUPS: tuple[str, ...]`; `ROLES: tuple[str, ...]`; `DECIDING: tuple[str, ...]`; `AGAIN_WORDS = "Run again"`; `entry(key: str) -> CheckEntry` (`KeyError` for another key). `STATES = ("Not run yet", "Running", "Passed", "Look closer", "Failed", "Waiting for a prior", "Waiting for a posterior", "Waiting for the simulations", "Waiting for the audit", "Not compared", "Does not apply", "Done")`; `Reading(state: str, words: str = "", counts: bool = True)`; `read(key: str, manifest: Mapping) -> Reading`; `Comparison(difference: float | None, state: str, sentence: str, message: str)`; `compare(*, unusable: int, total: int, scope: str, audit_fraction: float | None, accepted: Mapping | None = None, batches: int | None = None, prior_words: str = "the prior in use", kept_set: bool = True) -> Comparison`; `first_masked(text: str) -> tuple[int, int, int, str] | None` (the first `[chi] masked probes` record of a log, by `readings.read`); `check_lineage.read_text(folder: str, name: str = "log.txt") -> str | None` (a record's log read by its folder, never by the store's lookup; counted); `verdict_sentence(key: str, manifest: Mapping, *, others_open: int = 0) -> str` and `table_words(key: str, manifest: Mapping, *, units: Mapping[str, str] = {}) -> TableWords | None` (Task 5). `RecordRead(folder: str, manifest: Mapping)`; `read_records(store, kinds: Sequence[str] = ("diagnostic", "calibration")) -> tuple[RecordRead, ...]`; `Held(prior: str | None, posterior: str | None, posterior_prior: str | None, simulation: str | None, chi: bool | None, config: Mapping | None, configured: Mapping | None, rotated: bool, narrowed: bool, nadrowski: bool, forcing: bool, pair_refused: str, set_counts: tuple[int, int, int] | None, set_done: bool, prior_words: str, posterior_words: str)`; `CheckRow(key: str, state: str, line: str, when: datetime | None, record: RecordRead | None, earlier: tuple[str, ...], reason: str)`; `rows(held: Held, records: Sequence[RecordRead], histories: Sequence[Mapping]) -> tuple[CheckRow, ...]` (in `CHECKS` order); `headline(rows, *, held) -> tuple[str, str]` (the headline, its sentence); `ChecksSummary(passed: int, of: int, failed: bool, look_closer: bool, open: bool, can_check: bool, second_line: str, headline: str, sentence: str)`; `summary(rows, *, held) -> ChecksSummary`; `pre_run_fact(rows) -> Fact`; `pre_run_question(rows, *, start: str = "Start training") -> tuple[str, str, str] | None` (title, text, the other answer).
- **R-check-reading (Task 3).** `InferenceScreen.read_checks() -> None`, the one reading of what Checks needs (manifests only; at a showing, a run's end and a store change; never while a run is live; a counted reading), held as `InferenceScreen.checks_held`; `chain.step_states(..., checks=ChecksSummary)` fed by it, for the first time.

## How to run this plan

- **Re-planned by the owner on 11 November 2026.** From Task 5b on, the section "Re-plan of 11 November 2026 (the owner's)", just before Task 6, overrides what this section says of the execution order, the preparation, the per-task loop, the departures and the hand-over (departure 468).
- **The plan is committed when it is written**, with the design's rows 398 onward, for the owner's approval; a change the owner asks for, and any later correction, is its own commit. Nothing is built before the owner approves it; the tree is clean before Task 0.
- **Execution order:** 0, 1, 2, 3, …, 14, one task at a time, as "The task outline" orders them and says why.
- **Controller tasks** (0 and 14) are run by the controller. The rest are built by one fresh implementer each (sonnet), from ONE brief.
- **Preparation of every building task.** Before a task is dispatched, after the task before it is complete: for every task that touches threads, the run, the engine or what a run is handed (every task but Task 1), one skeptic (opus) reads this head, the task's section, the design's sections it cites and the code at the head, builds the task as a STAND-IN in a scratch copy of the tree against the REAL devices and today's real panels, runs the section's tests there (each seen red for its stated reason, then green) and the one-run faults that show each assertion can fail, and writes the task's brief, `stage-5/briefs/task-NN-brief.md`, as ONE document (the exact text of every test and of every line the section leaves to choice, the commits, the counts the gate should show). What the trial run finds that a person would meet and no test sees is brought to the controller, ruled, written INTO the brief with a test line, and the brief walked again end to end. An implementer is never given "the brief, and these overrides". Task 1 (tests only) is prepared by one reader (sonnet) who writes the brief from the readers' reports and runs nothing.
- **The per-task loop (the owner's rule).** On an implementer's DONE: no python alive, tree clean, the real `Artifacts/` listed; the task's one-process fast gate in the background AND the review at the same moment. The review is three readers: seat one (sonnet) the brief against what was built; seat two (opus) the product before against after, on the real devices, drawn where it draws; seat three (opus) the tests and the faults they would miss, its fault runs after the gate has exited (it reads first, ends its turn with "waiting to run", and is given the machine when the gate has exited). An Important finding goes to the task's skeptic (opus), resumed, with the controller's conditional rulings in a file. A task is complete when its review is clean AND its gate is green on the same head. One fix round: it waits for the gate to exit, is written and walked by the skeptic, built by an implementer as its own commit, re-read by one reader (sonnet) while a fresh gate runs. What the re-read leaves open is ruled, carried to a named task, or brought to the owner at the exit.
- **The last review** over the whole stage (three readers: the product as one piece, the tests as one piece, the sorting of every deferred finding) with ONE fix wave, then the exit with the owner (Task 14).
- **The real-screen run** (`QT_QPA_PLATFORM=windows pytest -m display`) after the gate of any task that adds or changes a display-marked test or the start-up path, and at the exit; started as a loop that tries every few seconds until no reader's script is alive.
- **The owner's questions** a task meets are asked while the previous gate runs, since the question waits for an answer; one at a time, in plain words, with a picture where it helps and a recommendation; also written in the closing message, since the owner often follows over Remote Control.
- **After each gate:** tree clean; the real `Artifacts/` unchanged (428 entries at stage 4's exit, its kinds `diagnostics`, `fdt`, `priors`, `simulations`, no `fits/`); no `sbi-logs/`; `Resources/Plans` as it was; `Resources/Models` holds `SHM.json` and `SHM2.json` only; the real `PRISM.ini` unchanged (3,296 bytes, sha256 79BF94DF…C83E) and its folder holding `PRISM.ini` and `PRISM.ini.bak-preband`; the desktop's four names and no `PRISM.lnk`; the gate's line in the ledger with its count and time.
- **Every agent** is given its model by name (Sonnet where reading is enough); one that runs scripts leaves no process behind and reads the process list before any run that builds PRISM's whole window or imports torch; a `cd` inside a Bash call ends back at the repository's root; no python at all beside another python; a test that reads state the whole process shares takes a reading first; never write into the repository while an implementer or a picture agent works there; GNU sed reads `\c` in a replacement as a control character. Times in the ledger are read from `date`.
- **Usage (the owner's rule of 16 October).** The controller reads the usage card at the start of every session and before every dispatch, tells the owner where the weekly bar stands, does not count on extra usage, starts nothing that cannot be finished below about 90 % of the weekly bar, and stops at a clean state (nothing half-built, the record written). A chat hands over to a new one (STATE, the ledger, a prompt) after about two or three tasks, at two thirds of its room, or when the usage rule says stop, whichever comes first.
- **When what is built departs from the design:** the task's report says so; the controller asks the owner if it touches something the owner decided, and adds a row to "Departures from the design" here, in the design's section 8 and in the ledger, with what it costs if it is wrong.
- **The ledger** is `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-06-window-redesign/progress.md`; stage 5's sections stand just above "## Engine changes landed"; never add a line above the lines that begin "- HAND-OFF"; `docs/STATE.md` is updated at the end of every session.

## Departures from the design

Rows 1 to 397 stand (section 8 of the design; this plan's predecessors). From 398, each with what it costs if it is wrong. "Owner" names the owner's answer where one decided it.

## The task outline

The controller's outline, binding as to scope and order; each task's section below fills it. The step keys are `keys.STEPS`'s: `checks` (step 4), `infer` (step 5), `narrowing` (step 6). Carried items are named by their ids in `reader-f-carried.md`; the departures by number.

**Order, and why.** The engine (2) first, so every later gate runs on its final lines. The checks' rules (3) before anything shows a check, and with them the chain's and Home's words and Training's pre-run line, which need no page. The pieces (4) before any page, because the Checks forms and Inference share them (a file row, a table of rows, two names, a result region, a list beside a detail). Then Checks: the list, a check's page and every result block (5); the forms and runs of the deciding checks (6) and of the informing checks and tools (7); the two checks that run in a scratch store (8); the comparison after simulating (9), which needs the run's model and check 3's page. Then Inference (10), because the round opens on the observation it makes; the round's page (11); plans (12) on the finished forms, all built by Task 11; the kept round after a reopening (13), which needs the round's page, the observation's link and the plans' fill, since its "Continue from batch N" fills the round's form through `fill_forms`, which knows the round only from Task 12; the exit (14).

**Task 0 (controller): workspace and baseline.** `stage-5/` (`plan-drafts/` exists; `briefs/`, `reports/`, `reviews/`, `gates/`, `tools/`, `exit/`); the gate tools copied from `s4\` to `s5\` and pointed at `stage-5/gates/` (`gate.sh`, `ground.ps1`, `card\card-gate.ps1`, `exit_fence.py`, `task01_test_names.py`), labels `s5taskNN-<sha>`, after a fix `s5taskNN-fix1-<sha>`; text copies in `stage-5/tools/`. The baseline is the quiet gate of record on `850dc23` (3572 passed, 11 skipped, 5 deselected; 3588 collected), HEAD above it documents only. The digests recorded before Task 2 (equal to `stage-4/gates/digests-at-exit.json`). The carried-over list gains the stage-5 marks reader E's section H lists. The loop written once in the ledger. The real `Artifacts/` listing taken.

**Task 1: The front (tests only; no product change).** Prepared by a reader (sonnet). In `tests/_runs.py`: the stand-in run honours `on_stage` (it can drive the card test's four stages and a check's stages), the run's lines of a check, a round's lines. In `tests/_fixtures.py`: `build_step_page(step=…)` stands a page in the slot it names; record writers in seconds for a diagnostic and a calibration (`kind`, `body.diagnostic`, `body.variant`, parents, `settings`, `results`, figures), an observation, a simulation set whose state file holds `chi_masked` and `batches_done`, a kept round (`build_fit_record` with a region, a parent posterior and an observation), a round's history (`write_history(word="Narrowing round", settings=…)`). One helper for each kind of record a test builds by hand today (reader E's fixtures section). The gate proves nothing was lost.

**Task 2: An observation's name and the six refusals (engine).** E4 (11.5, 11.8): `observation_name` on `simulated_inference` and `experimental_inference`, checked before anything is spent; E7's opt-in for the observation alone; the inference's own name taken is a refusal that costs one observation (8.1); the observation's note empty. E6's rest (11.7, B14): the input-use check's five plain errors (`core/diagnostics/ablation.py` about `:41`, `:105`, `:115`, `:130`, `:132`) and the third identifiability method's one (`identifiability.py` about `:238-241`) become `Refusal(..., field="posterior")`, raised before anything is spent, sentences unchanged (E11 rewords them in stage 9); the command line shows them as refusals. Departure 409's one keyword. `FIELDS` 114 to 115: `core/refusals.py`, `core/tool/fields.py`'s `FLAG`, `core/gui/fields.py`'s `CONTROL` (no control until Task 10), `tests/test_refusals.py`'s `BASE_KEYS` and count, `core/plans.py`'s class ("it belongs to one run", that reason's count 3 to 4). The engine's tests of an observation's name and of the six refusals written first. The code audit's list and "Engine changes landed". Digests equal; the last statement of `core/orchestrator.py` unchanged; line endings kept.

**Task 3: The checks' rules, the chain's words and Training's pre-run line.** 9.1 to 9.3 as pure functions with no toolkit and no torch (`check_catalogue.py`, `check_readings.py`, `check_lineage.py`): the twelve entries, the reading rules with B10's named constants, which records count (by id, never name; the newest decides; "an earlier run failed"; worst over the cells), the headline (C15 as the owner answered, S5-1) and its sentence table, the summary for the chain and Home (3.4, 3.6), the bands of the comparison (9.5) from counts, and the pre-run fact (C12) with its question after a "Failed". `tests/test_checks.py` asserts the headline over every combination of states on the function alone. The screen's one reading of the records Checks needs (manifests only; at a showing, a run's end and a store change; never live; joined to the counted readings), held for the chain: departure 55 closes (step 4's states and Home's "Next" from the summary). **Task 4: The pieces stage 5's pages need.** Built and tested on stand-in step pages before any real page, as stage 4's Task 7 did: a file row in a `Form` (`PathField` with `value_or_none`, `changed`, an error state), `ValuesGrid(scrolls=False)` with `changed`, a table of editable rows inside a form (add, remove with departure 403's question, a reading beside each row, a least width), two names in one block (an observation's and an inference's), a result region on the step page (a ruled table, figures, up to two next-step buttons that carry what they open), the list beside a detail with "All checks" and the switch to "in its place" below departure 412's width (the list's two columns beside a detail); `StepPage._others_pending` covers every earlier step (Checks says the pending changes of steps 1 to 3, Inference of steps 1 to 4, the round of steps 1 to 5). ## Task 1: The front (tests only; no product change)

**Implements:** the outline's Task 1; reader E's sections 5.1 (items 1, 3, 4, 6) and 8; the head's "Clocks and threads in tests". Prepared by a reader (sonnet). Carried-over rows: none change fate; the helpers get no rows (they are not tests).

**Files:**
- Modify: `tests/_runs.py`, `tests/_fixtures.py`, `tests/test_run_states.py`, `tests/test_artifact_store.py`

**Interfaces:** Produces, for every later task:
- `tests/_runs.StandIn(..., stages: Sequence[tuple[str, str, float | None]] = ())`: when the call it stands in for is handed `on_stage`, the stand-in calls `on_stage(stage, event, seconds)` for each tuple in order, on the calling (worker) thread, before it returns; at an event `"failed"` it then raises `RuntimeError("stand-in stage failed")` as the engine passes a failure on. A call handed no `on_stage` ignores `stages`.
- `tests/_runs.masked_line(unusable: int, total: int, batches: int, scope: str) -> str` and `masked_none(scope: str) -> str`: the engine's two `[chi] masked probes` forms exactly as `core/SBI/pipeline.py:224-236` builds them (the share to one decimal, `per batch lo-hi%, median m%` with fixed figures); `SCOPE_WHOLE = "every committed batch of the simulation cache"`, `SCOPE_PROCESS = "this process only (no simulation cache)"`, `scope_part(first: int) -> str` (the third form, with its `;`).
- `tests/_runs.CALIBRATION_LINES: tuple[str, ...]`: the four lines check 4's stages begin at (a `Generating training data` progress frame, `[patho] run total: …`, `SBC uniformity checks:`, `TARP: ATC=…`), as the engine prints them; `ROUND_LINES`: a narrowing round's (`[tsnpe] region …`, `[checkpoint] writing to <folder>`, a `masked_line`, `[fit] epoch 1: …`).
- `tests/_fixtures.build_step_page(cls=None, *, session=None, window=None, step: str = "training")`: the page stands in the slot of `step` (any key of `keys.STEPS`) and in that step's place of `_places`, and the stand-in page is built with that `step` (its `step="training"` is no longer fixed); today's callers pass nothing and are unchanged.
- Record writers, each through the store's own writer (`store.create`), in seconds, returning the record's id:
  `write_diagnostic(store, cfg, *, diagnostic: str, variant: str | None = None, parents: dict | None = None, settings: dict | None = None, results: dict | None = None, figures: Sequence[str] = (), cell: str | None = None, created: datetime | None = None, name: str = "", note: str = "") -> str`;
  `write_calibration(store, cfg, *, posterior: str, prior: str, results: dict | None = None, figures: Sequence[str] = (), created: datetime | None = None, name: str = "") -> str` (`results` defaults to a passing verdict at 2,000 datasets, 200 points, 1,000 samples, seed 1840273);
  `write_observation(store, cfg, *, name: str = "", digest: str = "0123456789abcdef", mode: str | None = None, width: int | None = None) -> str`;
  `write_simulation_set(root, identity, batches, *, complete=False, chi_masked: Sequence[tuple[int, int]] | None = None)` (the state file gains `chi_masked` as the engine writes it, one `[masked, total]` per batch);
  `build_fit_record(..., region: dict | None = None)` (a kept round: `region` as `region_to_json` writes it, with `level` and `dims`; `parents` then names `prior`, `simulation`, `parent_posterior`, `observation`);
  `write_history(folder, **fields)` already writes any word; its docstring gains a round's example (`word="Narrowing round"`, `settings={...}` naming `parent_posterior` and `observation`).
  Each figure named in `figures` is a small PNG drawn on a bare `Figure`, as `build_fdt_record` draws one. `created` is written as the store writes it (UTC with its offset).

**Interfaces:** Produces R-checks, R-check-reading, R-check-history (shapes below and in the registry), `chain.next_step(..., checks=)`, `HomeFacts.checks`. The three pure modules consume `core.gui.texts.date_text` and `core.gui.run.readings.read` only; `InferenceScreen` alone calls `record_words.label_of`, `core.artifacts.manifest.config_from_cfg`, `core.diagnostics.probes._configured` (called, not copied), `core.SBI.run_guards._assert_prior_used_matches_posterior` (called in a `try`, not copied) and `training_checkpoint.peek`, and hands their results to `Held` as plain values.

**Named training code:** none edited; none called.

Rules the brief must hold:
- **The catalogue (9.1).** `CHECKS` in key order `band, audit, comparison, calibration, input_use, identifiability, pin_down, timing, fixed_number, card_test, drive_study, across_prior` (numbers 1 to 12), each `name` and `sentence` word for word from 9.1's table, `group` one of `GROUPS = ("Before the run", "After simulating", "After the fit", "Tools")`, `role` one of `ROLES = ("Decides", "Informs", "Tool")`; `DECIDING` the first six keys. `button`: band "Run the frequency-band check", audit "Run the audit", calibration "Run the calibration", input_use "Run the input-use check", identifiability "Run the identifiability check", comparison "" (no button), the rest "Run this check"; `AGAIN_WORDS = "Run again"`. `stop_words`: calibration "Stop the calibration…", audit "Stop the audit…", the rest "Stop this check…". `kind`: calibration "calibration"; comparison, timing, card_test ""; the rest "diagnostic". `diagnostic`/`variant`: band `probes`/`band`, audit `probes`/`mask`, input_use `ablation`/None, identifiability `identifiability`/`rotation`, pin_down `identifiability`/`jacobian`, fixed_number `sbc`/None (its variants `pooled`, `k<N>`), drive_study `probes`/`drive`, across_prior `identifiability`/`laplace`. The list shows each group's checks in number order under the group's heading. `CHECKS` is built positionally, and every function of the three modules takes a check's key positionally, so no literal reaches a keyword named `key` or `field` (the head's two scans).
- **The reading rules (9.2), as named constants of `check_readings.py`, each with a `#:` line giving its reason in words:** `BAND_LEAST_REPEATS = 24`, `BAND_CV_MAX = 0.2`, `BAND_SNR_MIN = 3.0`, `BAND_SUP_MIN = 0.5`, `BAND_PEAK_WINDOW = 0.1` (the thresholds that define "holds"; a record made with others measured something else); `AUDIT_EXPECTED_SHARE = 0.37`, `AUDIT_FINE_POINTS = 8.0`, `AUDIT_FAILED_POINTS = 12.0`, `AUDIT_LEAST_BATCHES = 100` (the runbook's reading, which assumes 100 batches); `COMPARISON_FINE_POINTS = 3.0`, `COMPARISON_FAILED_POINTS = 5.0` (the owner's limits); `CALIBRATION_LEAST_DATASETS = 2000`, `CALIBRATION_LEAST_POINTS = 200`, `CALIBRATION_LEAST_SAMPLES = 1000`; `INPUT_USE_PARTS = ("A1_mean", "D3_bimodality")`, `INPUT_USE_LEAST_ROWS = 200_000`, `INPUT_USE_LEAST_SWEEP = 33`. `read(key, manifest)` holds 9.2's table exactly; "Look closer" never counts toward the headline; a record below its least size reads `Reading("Not run yet", …, counts=False)` whose words are "Run only at a reduced size (40 test datasets; the usual is 2,000), which does not count." (the audit: "(12 batches; the usual is 100)"; the band: "(8 simulations per point; the usual is 24)"; input use: "(1,000 simulations read; the usual is 200,000)"). The module imports `core.gui.run.readings` and `core.gui.texts` only, beside the standard library.
- **`compare` (9.5),** from the two counts, never the rounded share: `difference = abs(unusable / total − audit_fraction) × 100` in points, unrounded. `audit_fraction is None`: "Waiting for the audit", sentence "There is no audit of prior retrain_prior to compare with. The simulation set holds 40.1 % unusable driven recordings. Run the audit in step 4, Checks, when this run has ended."; `total == 0` or the scope is neither whole-set scope: "Not compared", sentence "Not compared: the count covers 240 batches, not the whole simulation set; the first 760 were saved without their counts." (or "Not compared: no driven recordings were counted."); `difference <= COMPARISON_FINE_POINTS`: "Passed", sentence "Unusable driven recordings 40.1 %. The audit read 37.9 %; within 3 points is fine."; `<= COMPARISON_FAILED_POINTS`: "Look closer", the same sentence form "… differ by 3.8 points; up to 5 is a reason to look closer.", message "The simulation set and the audit differ by 3.8 points. Look closer before leaving the fit to run: step 4, Checks, shows the per-batch figures of both."; above: "Failed", the sentence form "… differ by 5.2 points; beyond 5 the advice is to stop.", message, in the fit-saving design's words (departure 130): with a kept set "The advice is to stop the fit and find out why. The fit is saved after every epoch, so a stop keeps it and loses at most the epoch in progress; the training can be resumed later."; with `kept_set=False` "The advice is to stop the fit and find out why. This training keeps no simulation set, so a stop loses its simulations and its fit." An `accepted` handed (the acceptance of the audit that counts now; a caller drops one of another audit, so it lapses) turns "Look closer" into "Passed", the sentence gaining "A difference of 3.8 points was accepted on 3 Oct 2026, 09:14." Never raises; never divides by nought.
- **Which records count (9.3).** `RecordRead(folder: str, manifest: Mapping)`; `read_records(store, kinds=("diagnostic", "calibration")) -> tuple[RecordRead, ...]` reads each manifest once from the folder `store.list(kind)` hands back (`Summary.path / "manifest.json"`), never through the store's lookup by id (departure 414); a manifest that cannot be read is passed over and counted in `Held`'s words, never raised. `read_text(folder, name="log.txt") -> str | None` reads a record's log by its folder, never through `store.read_log`, which rescans every manifest of the kind (departure 414); both are counted readings. `Held` (frozen), every field a plain value the screen computed: `prior`, `posterior`, `posterior_prior`, `simulation` (ids or None), `chi` (bool or None), `config` (the six fields of `config_from_cfg(session.cfg)` 9.3 row 1 compares), `configured` (`probes._configured(cfg)` with frequency response, else None), `rotated`, `narrowed`, `nadrowski`, `forcing` (bools), `pair_refused` (the engine guard's sentence for the prior in use against the posterior, "" when it passes), `set_counts` (`(unusable, total, batches)` summed from `peek`'s `chi_masked` when its length equals `batches_done`, else from the posterior's log's first masked line, `first_masked(read_text(<P's folder>))`, else None), `set_done` (bool), `prior_words`, `posterior_words` (`record_words.label_of`'s). Rows by 9.3: by id, never by name; the newest counting record decides, its state line "Passed, 12 Oct 2026, 14:05. An earlier run failed (11 Oct 2026, 09:30)." when an earlier counting one differs; the band by the newest counting record of each cell (`inputs["cell"]["path"]`), the worst state over the cells; checks 1, 2, 3 "Does not apply" with "Applies only with the frequency response." when `chi` is False; with no posterior in use, S is the set the newest training's history of this records folder names (live or last), and the comparison is that history's `health.comparison` (Task 9); no prior: every row "Waiting for a prior"; checks 4 to 6, 9, 12 with no posterior "Waiting for a posterior"; check 5 with no `simulation` and check 6 with `rotated` False read "Not run yet" with 9.4's reasons and count as not passed; 7 and 11 by row 1's rule, 9 and 12 by `parents["posterior"]`, 8 and 10 from the newest `check` entry of their key in `histories` ("Done" only from an entry whose outcome is "finished"). The date is the record's `created` through `date_text`; for the comparison, the set's completion (`created` plus `wall_seconds`).
- **The headline,** `headline(rows, *, held) -> tuple[str, str]`: "n of 6 deciding checks passed"; with a posterior trained without the frequency response "n of 3 deciding checks passed; 3 do not apply to this posterior" (S5-1); with no posterior always "of 6". The sentence, the first that applies, word for word: "Nothing can be checked yet. Build or load a prior in step 2." / "Failed: Calibration." (the names in check order, ", " between) / "Look closer: Unusable driven recordings: audit." / "Three of the six do not apply to a posterior trained without the frequency response." / "The other four are made after simulating and after the fit." / "Inference can be run now; 2 deciding checks are still open." ("1 deciding check is still open.") / "This posterior has passed every deciding check." ("… every deciding check that applies to it.").
- **The summary,** `summary(rows, *, held) -> ChecksSummary`: `passed`, `of` (6 or 3), `failed`, `look_closer`, `open` (a deciding check that applies is not "Passed"), `can_check` (a prior is in use), `second_line` (the first of "1 failed", "1 to look closer at", "4 wait for step 3", "2 not run yet"; "" for none), `headline`, `sentence`.
- **The chain (3.4, 3.6).** With `checks=None` step 4 reads as today ("Needs a prior" / "Needs a posterior" / "To come") and is never in hand: only the screen passes a summary, so the pure rule tests that pass none keep their words. With a summary, `step_states(..., checks: ChecksSummary | None = None, ...)` reads it: not `can_check`: To come, "Needs a prior"; `failed`: Error, "Error: 2 of 6 passed"; `look_closer`: Attention, "Attention: 2 of 6 passed"; `passed == of`: Done, "Done: 6 of 6 passed" ("Done: 3 of 3 passed"); else Now when in hand, To come otherwise, "Now: 2 of 6 passed". `_IN_HAND` gains "checks" between "training" and "infer", in hand only while `open`. `_NEXT["checks"]`: heading "Next: check the posterior", the summary's headline as its sentence, button "Open Checks": `chain.next_step(session, states, *, run=None, picks=None, offers=(), checks=None)` takes the summary, `HomeFacts` gains `checks`, and `MainWindow._home_facts` passes `inference.checks_summary()` when the screen is built (None otherwise). The step's tooltip and accessible name carry `second_line`. With a summary, step 4 no longer reads "Needs a posterior". A step of 4 to 6 with a change not applied reads "Changed, not applied" on that step alone and is never the step in hand, as stage 4 holds it for steps 1 to 3. For a model inference cannot take (stage 4's row 161) steps 4 to 6 keep the chain's words, and the reason that greys a page's main button is the page's own. The screen's `seed_offers` (stage 4's row 333) stays: its first opening starts from the window's last reading of the offers and reads none of its own.
- **The one reading,** `InferenceScreen.read_checks() -> None`: `read_records`, the history of the records folder in use, `peek` of S and, failing it, `read_text` of P's folder, the engine's guard, `probes._configured`, `config_from_cfg` and `record_words.label_of`, all only when no run is live (a reading asked while live keeps the last); held as `checks_held` (`held`, `records`, `histories`, `rows`, `summary`). One trigger per moment: a page's `read_disk` when a page that reads the checks (Checks, Training, the round) is in sight; else the screen's own at `run_ended` and `stale_store`; a second call in the same turn of the event loop reads nothing. `InferenceScreen.checks_summary() -> ChecksSummary` recomputes `summary(rows(held_now, …))` with the last reading and a `Held` of what the session holds now, built from the objects in memory alone (no disk; `pair_refused` and `set_counts` kept from the last reading for the same ids, else "" and None); `recompute_states` and `MainWindow._step_states` pass it as `checks=`. `COUNTED_READINGS` gains `"check_lineage": ("read_records", "read_text")`, `history`'s `"last_check"`, and `readings_of_a_run` counts `ArtifactStore.read_log` and `ArtifactStore.list` of `diagnostic` and `calibration`.
- **The pre-run line (C12, 8.4),** `pre_run_fact(rows) -> Fact`: both "Passed": `Fact("fine", "Frequency band: Passed, 3 Oct 2026, 14:02. Unusable driven recordings: audit: Passed, 3 Oct 2026, 14:40.")`; both "Does not apply": `Fact("info", "The two pre-run checks do not apply: they need the frequency response.")`; otherwise `Fact("warn", …)` naming those not passed, in order ("Unusable driven recordings: audit: Not run yet."), a "Failed" one adding "Starting will ask first." at the end. Never `err` or `lock`. `pre_run_question(rows, *, start: str = "Start training") -> tuple[str, str, str] | None`: None unless one reads "Failed"; else title f"{start} although a check failed?", the text "Frequency band: Failed, 3 Oct 2026, 14:02. Step 4, Checks, says why.", the other answer f"{start} anyway". `training_facts(..., checks: Sequence[Fact] = ())` places them after `memory`, before `others_pending`. `PosteriorPanel.read_disk` calls `screen.read_checks()`; `main_pressed` asks the question first through `ask(self, title, text, CANCEL, other)`, "Cancel" preselected, then the kept-trainings or from-zero question as today; the `new_run` retry of `_refusal` does not ask it again.

- Modify: `core/gui/widgets/form.py`, `core/gui/widgets/labeled_inputs.py`, `core/gui/widgets/param_grid.py`, `core/gui/panels/inference/step_page.py`, `core/gui/step_facts.py` (`TableWords`, `RemoveQuestion`, `parameter_units`), `core/gui/screens/inference_screen.py` (`open_with`), `tests/test_pages.py`, `tests/test_look.py`, `tests/test_run_states.py`, `tests/_fixtures.py` (the stand-in pages)

**Interfaces:** Produces R-pieces (the registry's signatures). Consumes `Form`, `Row`, `Fold`, `NameAndNote`, `RuleTable`, `MessageRow`, `Sentence`, `Columns`, `FigureStack`, `ask`, `design.scaled`, `painted_icon`.

**Named training code:** none.

Rules the brief must hold:
- **A file row.** `PathField` gains `value_or_none() -> str | None` (a blank or all-space path is None), `set_value(value: str | None)`, `changed = Signal()` (at the end of editing and when "Browse…" chooses), `set_error(sentence: str | None)` (the outline of the look's fields in error, the sentence under the row as `Row.set_line` draws it); its focus proxy is its line edit. `Form.add_path(field_key, field, *, usual=None, folded=False, help_text="", label=None) -> Row`: as `add`, and the row's stops are the edit then "Browse…".
- **A grid on a page.** `ValuesGrid(parent=None, *, scrolls: bool = True)`: with `scrolls=False` no nested scroll area, dressed as `BoundsGrid(scrolls=False)` is (right-aligned, the number width, equal-width digits), `changed = Signal()`; its problems in the window's words: "No values: choose a cell file, or type them." and "Type a value for T." (the parameter's on-screen name).
- **A table of rows,** `RowTable(make_row: Callable[[], QWidget], *, typed: Callable[[QWidget], bool], question: RemoveQuestion, add_words: str, least_width: int)` in `form.py`: `rows() -> list[QWidget]`, `add_row() -> QWidget`, `remove(row) -> None`, `set_reading(row, kind: str, sentence: str)` (a `MessageRow` on the line under its row: at the window's least width there is no room beside a row), `clear_readings()`, `changed = Signal()`, `stops() -> list`. `RemoveQuestion(title, sentence, keep, remove)`, a frozen dataclass of `step_facts.py`, so a page's words stay toolkit-free. Each row ends with a remove button (the close glyph, `objectName` "iconButton", its tool tip and accessible name the caller's); a press of it, by a click or by Enter (the window-wide rule stands, departure 403), removes a row for which `typed(row)` is False at once, and otherwise asks `ask(self, question.title, question.sentence, question.keep, question.remove)`, the keep answer preselected; Escape and the frame's close keep the row. `Form.add_table(field_key, table, *, label=None, usual=None) -> Row`: a row whose `value()` is the list of the rows' values and whose least width is the table's.
- **Two names in one block.** `OtherName(key: str, kind: str, label: str, required: bool)`; `NameAndNote(kind, *, required, store=None, read_after_ms=READ_AFTER_MS, label: str = "Name", other: OtherName | None = None)`: with `other`, three rows in the order label, the other's label, "Note"; `rows` holds `"name"`, `other.key`, `"note"`; each name is checked against its own kind (`assert_name_free(kind, …)`, `assert_name_free(other.kind, …)`) 300 ms after its last keystroke and by `check_now()`; `other_value() -> str`, `other_fact() -> Fact | None` (a required one left blank: `Fact("err", "Type a name for the observation.", "a name is needed")` with the other's own words); `clear()` empties all three; never read while a run is live.
- **A result region,** `ResultRegion` in `result_region.py`, held by every `StepPage` as `self.result`, standing in the body after `in_use` and before the how-link: `set_sentences(facts: Sequence[Fact])` (message rows), `set_table(table: TableWords | None)` (a `RuleTable`, three rules, numbers right-aligned, every row shown, a heading row spanning the table's width, the caption above and the footnote below in the small type), `set_pictures(pictures: Sequence[tuple[str, Path]])` (each PNG a captioned picture fitted to the width, light in both themes), `set_next_steps(steps: Sequence[NextStep])` (at most two plain buttons; a third is a `ValueError`), `clear()`, `stops() -> list`; hidden while empty. `NextStep(words: str, step: str, carries: Mapping = {})`. A press emits `next_pressed(<the NextStep>)`, since the page needs its `carries`; the page calls `screen.open_with(step.step, step.carries)`. `TableWords(columns: tuple[str, ...], rows: tuple[tuple[str, ...], ...], right: frozenset[int] = frozenset(), headings: frozenset[int] = frozenset(), caption: str = "", footnote: str = "")` lives in `step_facts.py` (pure). `region_stops()` of a page includes `self.result.stops()`.
- **What a next step carries (B4: a next-step button acts).** `InferenceScreen.open_with(step: str, carries: Mapping) -> None`: `navigate.emit("inference", step)`, then the page's `take_carried(carries)`, called only on a page that has one (`getattr`: an old page has none); `StepPage.take_carried(carries: Mapping) -> None` does nothing unless a page overrides it. What it carries that the step holds is loaded by that step's own load, after the one question when something held would be let go, before the step is shown; what it carries that is a pick of the step's form (an observation for the round) is selected in its row. A next step never leaves a step reading "Changed, not applied".
- **A list beside its detail,** `ListAndDetail(listing: QWidget, *, beside_width: int, back_words: str = "All checks")` in `list_detail.py`: `show(detail: QWidget | None)`, `fit(width: int)` (called by the page body's `fit`, the width the scroller hands), `beside() -> bool`, `back_pressed = Signal()`, `stops() -> list`. No detail: the listing alone, whole. A detail and `width >= beside_width`: the listing told `set_narrow(True)` beside the detail. A detail and a narrower width: the detail alone with the plain link `back_words` above it. The switch reads the width and never the small layout (departure 412: the small layout is a rule on height). The caller passes `beside_width` already scaled.
- **A form handed to the foot.** `StepPage.use_form(form: Form) -> None`: `self.form` and `self.controls` become `form`; its `edited` and `joined` connected once; the keys ordered again; `redraw()`. A page that holds several forms (Checks) hands the one in sight. The step page's own form stays its first.
- **Other steps' changes.** `_others_pending` covers every earlier step that holds something: its `held` gains `"training": record_words.label_of(session.posterior)` and `"infer": record_words.label_of(session.observation)` beside "model" and "prior" ("checks" holds nothing and is never pending), so a later step says "Step 3, Training, has changes that are not applied. This run uses what is in use: retrain_posterior." A change not applied is said only on the steps after it, never on its own step's "Before you start", and only for the steps actually pending.
- **The stage-4 exit's answers (the head's line).** The foot's lead words come from the blocks a page's facts carry, as stage 4 built them: a block that waiting will not lift (a record made for another model, trained without its simulations, without the rotation, a model or a kind of observation a check does not apply to) reads "Cannot start: …"; one that a run's end or a press elsewhere will lift ("needs a prior", "needs a posterior", the live-run sentence, a field to edit) "Cannot start yet: …"; each page's blocks join `step_facts`' table of those that waiting will not lift where they belong, in the task that writes them. The low window keeps "Show all N" and "Show fewer".
- **Units.** `step_facts.parameter_units(rescale_kinds: Mapping[str, str], units: Mapping[str, str], assumed: Iterable[str] = ()) -> dict[str, str]`: a parameter's unit by its kind ("length" the configuration's length unit, "time" its time unit, "force" its force unit, a temperature assumed "K"), "" where it has none; the caller reads `cfg.rescale_idx` and `sim_config`'s `length_unit`, `time_unit`, `force_unit`.
